#### R1 6.X Overall design options

These are whole-design options drawn from the SOLUTION_RESEARCH section of the research notes (approaches 1-6). They combine the conflict resolutions above; they are not a roadmap and they do not choose a branch structure. Options A and B are stages of one unification path (A is B's first stage); Option C (keep both stacks) is the only alternative to that path.

```
                 +-----------------------------------------------------------+
 Option B shape: |  shared core                                              |
                 |  finding contract (v2 + lens registry) | panel resolver   |
                 |  dispatcher + adapters | run manifest   | clusterer       |
                 |  citation checks | decider hook | telemetry | verdict     |
                 +--------+----------+-----------+-----------+---------------+
                          |          |           |           |
                    reflect pre/  workflow    spec review   PR code review,
                    post module   tasklist    (brainstorm,  rf-qa replacement
                                  module      design)       (future modules)
```

##### Option A: findings-v2 as the single contract, v1 as a strict subset, on the existing two code stacks

**Description:** Keep the package layout; make findings-v2 the contract that every consumer writes; accept findings-v1 lines as a subset; fix R1-D1..R1-D16 in place; add lenses by editing the closed tuples.
**How it works:** `findings_v2.py` gains the block-level v1 rules the code lacks (empty block, `prior` lines, `reflect-post`) and its docstring is corrected; `LENSES` and the category sets grow by code change; the ledger parses v2; reflect keeps the debate and 1.8.0; the cluster path stays telemetry for reflect.

| Aspect | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | L |
| Risk | Medium |
| Reuse of existing code | High: everything stays; `findings_v2`, `cluster`, `panel`, `manifest`, ledger |
| Files/systems affected | `findings.py`, `findings_v2.py`, `ledger.py`, `merge_cmd.py`, `cluster.py`, briefs, tests that pin v1 text |
| Pros | Smallest path to one contract; E1 and E3 code both survive |
| Cons | Every new lens or category is a code change (`LENSES` and `CATEGORIES` are closed tuples); a code-review or rf-qa finding still needs new severity and location kinds; `merge_round` stays single-document |

##### Option B: a new superset core with pluggable lens and category namespaces and several location types

**Description:** Build the shared core as data-driven registries: lens (candidate kind, severity vocabulary, categories, primary location kind, blocking predicate, id grammar), location union (anchor, citations with role, file and section, multi-file observation with `in_diff`), launch adapters per environment, review kinds beyond first and re-review, and fix and verdict contracts as data; findings-v1 and findings-v2 become profiles of the core.
**How it works:** start from the Option A code (v2 is the natural base, 01 §18), then move the closed tuples into registries; thin modules (reflect pre and post, workflow tasklist, spec review, PR review, rf-qa replacement) declare only their lens, brief slots and gate strength; the core exposes the 13 exposures R1-X1..R1-X13 of research 10 §5.

| Aspect | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | XL |
| Risk | Medium-High |
| Reuse of existing code | High (v2 code, `PROFILES` pattern, reflect profile generalised) but a new registry layer |
| Files/systems affected | the whole `cli/review/` package, reflect skill and templates, `pr_submit` severity router and rubric (one table both sides read), rf-qa agents, telemetry, tier file |
| Pros | Once R1-M1 delivers exposures R1-X1..R1-X14, a new consumer is data plus a thin module (the future-use exposure list; the non-core prerequisites are in the R1-M4 Dependencies); one rule per lens (R1-C16); covers PR location kinds and rf-qa file-and-section locations |
| Cons | Largest design surface; the registry layer is untested; PR and rf-qa seams have open owner choices (finder versus S2b verifier, R1-G-80; rubric versus router authority, R1-G-78) |

##### Option C: keep both stacks and add adapters

**Description:** Do not unify; add `card_convert`-style adapters from each producer to each consumer and share only the panel resolver and the manifest.
**How it works:** document loops keep v1 and the ledger; reflect keeps v2 and the cluster path; PR review and rf-qa get their own adapters.

| Aspect | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Effort | M |
| Risk | Medium |
| Reuse of existing code | Complete |
| Files/systems affected | New adapters only |
| Pros | Fastest; no regression exposure on the 1,182 gate items (12 §8) |
| Cons | The four limits stay in nine places; R1-D1, R1-D5, R1-D9 and the two invalid-block policies remain; every adapter repeats severity-word guesses (rf-qa `IMPORTANT` inverts under `card_convert`); two merge-ledger implementations and a third reflect merge path stay |

**Evidence:**
- The package already holds two stacks with different jobs: v1 decides (findings, ledger, merge, verdict), v2 only flags and records (findings_v2, card_convert, cluster, verify-citations, manifest); v2 imports private v1 names, so v1 edits propagate (research notes PATTERNS; 01 §2) [CODE-VERIFIED].
- Today no consumer exercises the E1 kernel in production and the #346 merge and ledger CLI has exactly one in-repo caller (09 §5, §7) [CODE-VERIFIED]; migrating consumers is therefore not blocked by existing production callers except the tasklist post gate through `ensemble.py` (R1-C17).
- The core would have to expose open lenses, per-lens categories and severities, a location union, multi-artifact candidates with a pluggable clustering key, roles separate from models, more review kinds, fix and verdict contracts as data, provenance for grading, and model-role mapping and strata as data (10 §5 R1-X1..R1-X13) [CODE-VERIFIED reading]; today's closed `LENSES`, spec-only `CATEGORIES`, three-level severity, single-document anchor clustering, model-only templates, `--kind first|re-review` and sonnet/haiku strata each block at least one future consumer (10 §6).
- Reflect's frozen contract 1.8.0 permits sharing resolution, cards, clustering, citation checks, calibration coverage and manifests, but not the merge decision or contract writer (07 §10 Q8) [CODE-VERIFIED].
- External context [web]: production AI review systems separate finders from a coordinator that deduplicates, verifies against source and applies a severity rubric, and dedup on typed location and category rather than text similarity (https://blog.cloudflare.com/ai-code-review; web-04 R1-D1, R1-D2); SARIF models location as a union of physical, logical and snippet-hash kinds (web-04 R1).

**Assessment summary:** Options A and B are two stages of one unification path (B starts from A's code, so A is B's first stage), and Option C is the only real alternative to that path and the only choice that leaves the measured defect classes unchanged; the comparison is therefore between "unify, in stages" and "do not unify", not three independent designs. The conflict resolutions above are consistent with building toward Option B through Option A. Decided by evidence: R1-C01, R1-C02, R1-C04, R1-C06 to R1-C12, R1-C15 to R1-C20. Resolved by adversarial debate and left `partial`: R1-C03, R1-C05, R1-C13 and R1-C14. Option B depends on those four: the launch adapters and the class-launch grammar on R1-C03 (owner item R1-O-2, the target environment), the decider the registry carries on R1-C05, the ledger rounds model on R1-C13, and the template and reviewer-count defaults on R1-C14 (Decision R1-D-1).

#### Options Comparison

| Criterion | Option A: findings-v2 single contract, v1 subset | Option B: new superset core, pluggable lenses and location types | Option C: keep both stacks with adapters |
|---|---|---|---|
| Effort | L | XL | M |
| Risk | Medium | Medium-High (new registry layer, wider blast radius) | Medium (drift grows) |
| Maintainability | Good for existing lenses; every new lens is a code change in closed tuples | Best long term: lenses, categories, severities and predicates are data; one validator | Poor: limits and rules duplicated in nine places; two ledger paths and a third reflect merge path stay |
| Integration complexity | Medium: v1 consumers move to v2 parsing; ledger gains citations | High: touches package, skills, PR router, rf-qa agents, telemetry | Low per adapter, rising with each consumer |
| Reuse potential | High for reflect and document loops; low for PR and rf-qa without new kinds | High for all consumers by design (R1-X1..R1-X13) | Low: each consumer gets its own mapping |
| Future-consumer fit: PR code review | Weak: needs `in_diff`, five-tier severity, `line: null`, multi-file observations, closed PR reviewer set | Strong: location union and per-lens severity and predicate; seam choice (finder or S2b verifier) still open | Weak to medium: adapter can map fields but not the severity and merge semantics |
| Future-consumer fit: rf-qa replacement | Weak: `file:section` locations, IMPORTANT-as-blocking and fix-agent hand-off are missing | Strong for the review half; the 32 call sites that need an in-place fix keep a separate fix agent (Reviser) | Weak: severity words invert under today's word map |

**Condition on the Option B ratings.** The Medium-High risk of Option B is conditional: it holds only if the four debate-gated conflicts (R1-C-03, R1-C-05, R1-C-13, R1-C-14) are settled, and they ended `partial` with 26 unaddressed HIGH invariants in total (2 + 11 + 10 + 3, each debate's own numbering), and it assumes owners for two things nobody has named: approval of changes to the shared core (R1-O-4, R1-G-92) and the benchmark port (R1-G-59). With those open the risk is higher than the table reads. The effort and risk lines of the single conflicts in Section 6 are pre-debate ratings; the "Rating of the adopted resolution" lines of R1-C-03, R1-C-05, R1-C-11 and R1-C-14 give the adopted ones.

**Recommended design (not a roadmap):** build toward Option B through Option A's code. Start from findings-v2 as the single contract (R1-C01), keep the debate and contract 1.8.0 for reflect (R1-C11), and move the closed tuples into lens registries as new consumers need them. Option C is rejected because it leaves R1-D1, R1-D5, R1-D9, the opposite invalid-block policies and the third merge path in place.

#### R1 6.E Detail carried from the four adversarial debates

The adversarial results above stay as they are: the four debates stay `partial` and no winner changes. This subsection carries the decisions, residual risks, open items, invariant closures, test pins, scripts, coupled edit sites and rules that the debate outputs state and that an implementer needs, one sub-table per debate (6.E.1 R1-C-13, 6.E.2 R1-C-14, 6.E.3 R1-C-05, 6.E.4 R1-C-03). Each detail is the debate output's own statement in short form, not a new conclusion; where a debate output left a point open, the row says so. A row may group items that restate one point in several debate files; the `AO3 ids` column names every item of `qa/qa-anti-omission-report-3.md` that the row carries.

**Source cells.** Paths are relative to `reviews/adversarial/<slug>/`, with the line after the colon. Short names: PM = `proposal-merged.md`; VA = `proposal-a.md` (variant 1 original); VB = `proposal-b.md` (variant 2 original); DT = `adversarial/debate-transcript.md`; IP = `adversarial/invariant-probe.md`; DA = `adversarial/diff-analysis.md`; BS = `adversarial/base-selection.md`; ML = `adversarial/merge-log.md`; RP = `adversarial/refactor-plan.md`; RR = `adversarial/refactor-plan-review.md`; FF = `adversarial/round25-fault-finder-raw.md`; R1A = `adversarial/round1-advocate-A-statement.md`; R2A and R2B = `adversarial/round2-advocate-A-rebuttal.md` and `-B-rebuttal.md`; SP1 and SP2 = `adversarial/scorer-pass1-raw.md` and `-pass2-raw.md`. The slug folder is named in each sub-heading.

**Kind values.** decision, residual risk, open item, invariant closure, test pin, script, coupled edit site, rule.

##### R1 6.E.1 R1-C-13 ledger model (`reviews/adversarial/ledger-model/`)

| Item | Kind (decision / residual risk / open item / invariant closure / test pin / script / coupled edit site / rule) | Detail | Source (file:line in reviews/adversarial/<slug>/) | AO3 ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1-C13-01 Probe totals, gate result and the invariant register | residual risk | The Round 2.5 probe (an independent fault-finder against the consensus after Round 2; the orchestrator re-read the code behind R1-INV-001, 003, 004, 006, 009, 013 and found it accurate) gave 20 findings: 18 UNADDRESSED (10 HIGH, 7 MEDIUM, 1 LOW) and 2 ADDRESSED in principle (R1-INV-018, R1-INV-020); the fault-finder's own footer says "16 UNADDRESSED" but by its entries the count is 18. Gate result: CONVERGENCE BLOCKED by 10 HIGH UNADDRESSED invariants (R1-INV-001 to R1-INV-010); at `--depth standard` there is no Round 3, so they were not debated. The refactor plan carries the unresolved conflicts (R1-S-006, R1-C-013, R1-A-002, R1-A-004) and the unaddressed invariants (R1-INV-001 to R1-INV-017, R1-INV-019) as a visible register, not resolved by editing. Merge-log Change 20 adds the section "Invariant register" after Correctness: the restated sufficiency claim, a 20-row table (probe severity, probe status, finding, design status in the port, design status in option (i), closure rule), the four conditions of `closed-in-port` and F1 to F6. | PM:364; RP:17,183; ML:182-183 | L1-022, L5-010, L4-162 |

No row is `closed` until its acceptance test exists and passes: a `closed-*` entry means a closure rule is written into the proposal and names the planned acceptance test, it is not a verification. `closed-in-port` applies to R1-INV-003, R1-INV-004, R1-INV-005, R1-INV-006 and R1-INV-017 under four conditions: (1) the only writer of a round decision is the ported `close` and `decide`; (2) the `ledger revise` shim runs the ported `answer` and `begin`, not master `apply_revision`, which moves any open or regressed `applied` row to `resolved_check` without checking `blocking` (`ledger.py:328-339`); (3) demoted rows (Core item 4) stay outside the plan; (4) rows reopened through `reattack_of` enter the next plan. For option (i) these five items are `open`. | PM:366,399; ML:183,346; RP:186,302 | L1-023, L1-199, L4-164, L5-136 |
| R1-C13-03 R1-INV-005 (HIGH): an `unverified` FAIL names no ids; the clearing gate | invariant closure | Probe R1-INV-005: a would-be PASS with an uncleared `resolved_check` row is recorded as FAIL with `unverified: [lids]` and consumes a review number, but three places read only `brief_ids`: the merged report's `## Blocking findings` and `## Reviser brief` (`ledger.py:438-466`, `:487-493`) render "Blocking finding ids: none"; the next reviewer's instruction takes "one prior line per prior blocking finding id" from PRIOR (`document_review_loop_prompts.md:49,61`), so the unverified ids are not named; `_decision` returns REVISE below the cap (`:93-96`), so the reviser is sent into a fix cycle with an empty id list and review 3 ESCALATEs. Closure rule: render `unverified` ids in a separate merged-report section ("Verify these rows; answer each with a prior line and an evidence quote"), add them to the RE-REVIEW instruction, keep them out of `brief_ids`, and use a distinct RE-REVIEW decision (no reviser step) or state that REVISE with an empty id list is accepted; test: round 2 `unverified` FAIL, then round 3 with prior lines plus evidence reaches PASS (T-P5, T-P17). Port: `closed-in-port` (plan rows are the live blocking in-scope rows; an unanswered row is refused, not recorded). Option (i): open unless the `unverified` rule is adopted. Clearing gate it rests on: a `resolved_check` row clears only when a valid reviewer gave a `prior` line for it and did not re-report it (what `document_review_loop_prompts.md:44-48` already requires); a would-be PASS with any uncleared row is recorded as FAIL with `unverified: [lids]`, which consumes a review number, bounds the loop and removes the mutation problem; cost: a non-compliant model stalls into ESCALATE and the rate is unmeasured, so `unanswered_priors` telemetry would measure it first. | PM:381,572; FF:47-53; R2A:61 | L1-183, L6-087, L6-138 |
| R1-C13-04 R1-INV-010 (HIGH): the sufficiency claim is restated and a path-closure test is required | invariant closure | The unconditional claim that the loop "cannot certify PASS wrongly, cannot clear a row wrongly and always terminates" is withdrawn (false for the consensus package alone, F1 to F6). The claim made: no PASS and no clear without a positive, script-checked signal on the recorded-round path, given R1-INV-001 to R1-INV-009 closed. Branch enumeration (R1-INV-010): PASS is assigned only at `ledger.py:274` or in `defer` at `:358-360`; a row leaves `_brief_ids` by six branches: never created because a valid line is dropped while another reviewer's block is valid (`:150`); born or re-found non-blocking (`:187,213-218`); `resolved_check` at `:334` from a fix map that `ledger_cmd.py:64-70` applies even when `fm["ok"]` is False; `resolved` by silence (`:254-258`); `deferred` (`:353`); an out-of-band edit, since `load` checks only the schema (`:47-48`). Closure: a path-closure test that enumerates every writer of `status` and `blocking` and asserts each is gated (T-P10); design status `open` in both the port and option (i), with F4, F5 and F6 accepted as residual risks (R1-C13-05). | PM:371,386; IP:18,41; RP:186 | L1-177, L1-178, L1-188, L2-038, L5-132 |
| R1-C13-05 F4, F5, F6: accepted residual risks and their owners | residual risk | F4: a quorate panel with a dropped blocking line is accepted residual risk, owner the R1-C05 hook (the table in Core item 3 refuses a would-be PASS beside a dropped blocking line only for a non-valid reviewer). The base's "fail-closed by construction" is replaced by fail-closed on procedure only: a missing, malformed or rejected reply never yields PASS, but on meaning the shipped agent fails open (`finding-grader.md:17`, "Otherwise it is not blocking") and grades unverified evidence as if true (`:19`), so it can drop a real blocker (R5). | R1-C05-26 P1 and P2: why a grader's precision does not depend on panel size and where recall comes from | rule | P1: under the veto a spurious cluster blocks when any member is flagged, so with n reviewers that each over-flag it with probability q the chance it blocks is 1 - (1 - q)^n, which rises with n; under a grader the chance is the grader's false-block rate g, fixed by the rubric and the batch and independent of n. The formula is a model, not an E2 finding (reviewer flags are not independent, R1-X7, R1-X11). Assumptions: the grader's output depends only on the rendered batch (checked by a test) and batch position effects are controlled by seeded shuffling (R1-X9). Falsifier: on the E2 control package the grader's wrong-blocker count is not lower than the veto's on the same candidate sets, necessary and not sufficient (a grader that demotes everything meets it, R1-INV-019). P2: recall is the union's recall minus the grader's misses; the candidate rule admits every flagged and every MAJOR or CRITICAL cluster, singletons included; a grader cannot find a defect no reviewer reported; the pilot's 0 of 8 real-blocking recall under the veto is a candidate-generation question that R1-C14 leaves open; the grader's recall risk is a false negative, which canaries do not measure; real-blocking recall is already low (0.17 to 1.20 of 8 per run for replicated panels), so a floor that low cannot show a loss. | PM:489,491 | W1-184, W1-185 |
